 1 | from fastapi import APIRouter, HTTPException, BackgroundTasks
 2 | from models.scan_result import ScanRequest, ScanResponse
 3 | from services.repo_ingestion import ingest_repository
 4 | from services.code_parser import parse_codebase
 5 | import traceback
 6 | from utils.cache import get_session, set_session
 7 | 
 8 | router = APIRouter()
 9 | 
10 | def _embed_in_background(session_id: str, repo_path: str):
11 |     """Run embedding asynchronously after scan returns."""
12 |     try:
13 |         from services.embeddings import build_repo_embeddings
14 |         build_repo_embeddings(session_id, repo_path)
15 |         set_session(session_id, {"repo_path": repo_path, "embedded": True})
16 |         print(f"[Embeddings] ✅ Session {session_id} embedded successfully.")
17 |     except Exception as e:
18 |         print(f"[Embeddings] ⚠️ Failed for session {session_id}: {e}")
19 | 
20 | @router.post("/api/scan", response_model=ScanResponse)
21 | def scan_repository(request: ScanRequest, background_tasks: BackgroundTasks):
22 |     try:
23 |         # Step 1: Ingest Repo (clone + metadata)
24 |         ingest_data = ingest_repository(request.repo_url)
25 |         session_id = ingest_data["session_id"]
26 |         repo_metadata = ingest_data["repo"]
27 |         repo_path = ingest_data["repo_path"]
28 | 
29 |         # Step 2: Parse Codebase (file tree)
30 |         file_tree = parse_codebase(repo_path)
31 | 
32 |         # Step 3: Embed in background — don't block the response!
33 |         background_tasks.add_task(_embed_in_background, session_id, repo_path)
34 | 
35 |         return ScanResponse(
36 |             session_id=session_id,
37 |             repo=repo_metadata,
38 |             file_tree=file_tree,
39 |             status="scan_complete"
40 |         )
41 |     except ValueError as ve:
42 |         raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail=str(ve))
43 |     except Exception as e:
44 |         traceback.print_exc()
45 |         raise HTTPException(status_code=500, detail=f"Scan error: {str(e)}")
46 | 
47 | @router.get("/api/scan/{session_id}/status")
48 | def get_scan_status(session_id: str):
49 |     session_data = get_session(session_id)
50 |     if session_data:
51 |         embedded = session_data.get("embedded", False)
52 |         return {
53 |             "session_id": session_id,
54 |             "status": "scan_complete",
55 |             "progress": 100,
56 |             "ai_ready": embedded   # frontend can check this
57 |         }
58 |     else:
59 |         return {"session_id": session_id, "status": "scanning", "progress": 50, "ai_ready": False}

<file><path>codePulse-clean/backend/routes/analysis.py</path>
<content lines="1-139">
  1 | from fastapi import APIRouter, HTTPException
  2 | import os
  3 | from models.analysis_result import AnalysisResponse
  4 | from services.code_parser import parse_codebase
  5 | from services.complexity import calculate_heatmap, build_heatmap_tree
  6 | from services.dependency_graph import build_dependency_graph
  7 | from services.onboarding import generate_onboarding_data
  8 | from services.scale_simulator import compute_scale_profile
  9 | from utils.cache import get_session
 10 | import traceback
 11 | 
 12 | router = APIRouter()
 13 | 
 14 | def extract_all_routes(file_tree, current_path=""):
 15 |     routes = []
 16 |     for node in file_tree:
 17 |         node_path = f"{current_path}/{node['name']}" if current_path else f"/{node['name']}"
 18 |         if node["type"] == "folder":
 19 |             routes.extend(extract_all_routes(node["children"], node_path))
 20 |         else:
 21 |             api_routes = node.get("api_routes", [])
 22 |             for r in api_routes:
 23 |                 routes.append({
 24 |                     "method": r["method"],
 25 |                     "path": r["path"],
 26 |                     "file": node_path,
 27 |                     "line": 1
 28 |                 })
 29 |     return routes
 30 | 
 31 | @router.get("/api/analysis/{session_id}", response_model=AnalysisResponse)
 32 | def get_analysis(session_id: str):
 33 |     session_data = get_session(session_id)
 34 |     if not session_data:
 35 |         raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail="Session not found")
 36 |         
 37 |     repo_path = session_data["repo_path"]
 38 |     try:
 39 |         file_tree_root = parse_codebase(repo_path)
 40 |         file_tree = file_tree_root["children"]
 41 |         
 42 |         heatmap_files = calculate_heatmap(file_tree)
 43 |         heatmap_tree = {"name": "root", "children": build_heatmap_tree(file_tree)}
 44 |         dependency_graph = build_dependency_graph(file_tree)
 45 |         api_routes = extract_all_routes(file_tree)
 46 |         onboarding_path, suggested_questions = generate_onboarding_data(file_tree)
 47 |         
 48 |         total_files = len(heatmap_files)
 49 |         avg_complexity = int(sum(f["complexity"] for f in heatmap_files) / total_files) if total_files else 0
 50 |         health_score = max(0, 100 - (avg_complexity // 2))
 51 |         
 52 |         # Calculate languages
 53 |         lang_map = {}
 54 |         ext_to_lang = {
 55 |             '.py': 'Python',
 56 |             '.js': 'JavaScript',
 57 |             '.ts': 'TypeScript',
 58 |             '.tsx': 'TypeScript',
 59 |             '.jsx': 'JavaScript',
 60 |             '.html': 'HTML',
 61 |             '.css': 'CSS',
 62 |             '.go': 'Go',
 63 |             '.rs': 'Rust',
 64 |             '.java': 'Java',
 65 |             '.cpp': 'C++',
 66 |             '.c': 'C'
 67 |         }
 68 |         for f in heatmap_files:
 69 |             ext = os.path.splitext(f["name"])[1].lower()
 70 |             lang = ext_to_lang.get(ext, 'Other')
 71 |             lang_map[lang] = lang_map.get(lang, 0) + 1
 72 |         
 73 |         # Convert to percentages
 74 |         total_lang = sum(lang_map.values())
 75 |         languages = {k: (v / total_lang) * 100 for k, v in lang_map.items()} if total_lang else {}
 76 | 
 77 |         # Compute scale simulation profile
 78 |         scale_profile = compute_scale_profile(
 79 |             heatmap_files, api_routes, dependency_graph, 
 80 |             total_files, avg_complexity, languages
 81 |         )
 82 | 
 83 |         return AnalysisResponse(
 84 |             health_score=health_score,
 85 |             heatmap_files=heatmap_files,
 86 |             heatmap_tree=heatmap_tree,
 87 |             directory_tree=file_tree,
 88 |             languages=languages,
 89 |             dependency_graph=dependency_graph,
 90 |             api_routes=api_routes,
 91 |             total_files=total_files,
 92 |             avg_complexity=avg_complexity,
 93 |             onboarding_path=onboarding_path,
 94 |             suggested_questions=suggested_questions,
 95 |             scale_profile=scale_profile
 96 |         )
 97 |     except Exception as e:
 98 |         traceback.print_exc()
 99 |         raise HTTPException(status_code=500, detail="Internal server error during analysis")
100 | 
101 | from models.git_result import GitHistoryResponse
102 | from services.git_history import parse_git_history
103 | 
104 | @router.get("/api/analysis/{session_id}/git-history", response_model=GitHistoryResponse)
105 | def get_git_history(session_id: str):
106 |     session_data = get_session(session_id)
107 |     if not session_data:
108 |         raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail="Session not found")
109 |         
110 |     repo_path = session_data["repo_path"]
111 |     try:
112 |         history_data = parse_git_history(repo_path)
113 |         return GitHistoryResponse(**history_data)
114 |     except Exception as e:
115 |         traceback.print_exc()
116 |         raise HTTPException(status_code=500, detail="Internal server error extracting git history")
117 |

121 |     if not session_data:
122 |         raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail="Session not found")
123 |     from services.embeddings import build_repo_embeddings
124 |     build_repo_embeddings(session_id, session_data["repo_path"])
125 |     return {"status": "success", "message": "Embeddings created"}

132 |

<file><path>codePulse-clean/backend/routes/chat.py</path>
<content lines="1-29">
 1 | from fastapi import APIRouter, HTTPException
 2 | from models.chat_result import ChatRequest, ChatResponse
 3 | from services.embeddings import retrieve_context
 4 | from services.watsonx_client import chat_with_granite
 5 | 
 6 | router = APIRouter()
 7 | 
 8 | @router.post("/api/chat", response_model=ChatResponse)
 9 | def handle_chat(request: ChatRequest):
10 |     # 1. Retrieve RAG context
11 |     sources, context = retrieve_context(request.session_id, request.message)
12 |     
13 |     if not context:
14 |         context = "No direct codebase context found for this query."
15 |         sources = []
16 |         
17 |     # 2. Add active tab context
18 |     prompt = request.message
19 |     if request.active_tab:
20 |         prompt = f"[User is currently viewing the {request.active_tab} tab] {prompt}"
21 |         
22 |     # 3. Ask WatsonX
23 |     response_text = chat_with_granite(prompt, context)
24 |     
25 |     return ChatResponse(
26 |         response=response_text,
27 |         sources=sources,
28 |         model="meta-llama/llama-3-3-70b-instruct"
29 |     )
</content>
</file>
<file><path>codePulse-clean/backend/services/repo_ingestion.py</path>
<content lines="1-75">
 1 | import os
 2 | import uuid
 3 | import tempfile
 4 | import requests
 5 | from git import Repo
 6 | from utils.cache import set_session
 7 | 
 8 | def parse_github_url(url: str):
 9 |     parts = url.rstrip('/').split('/')
10 |     if len(parts) >= 2:
11 |         return parts[-2], parts[-1]
12 |     raise ValueError("Invalid GitHub URL")
13 | 
14 | def ingest_repository(repo_url: str):
15 |     owner, name = parse_github_url(repo_url)
16 |     
17 |     # Use GitHub REST API directly (no PyGitHub, no rate-limit auth needed for public repos)
18 |     github_token = os.getenv("GITHUB_TOKEN", "")
19 |     headers = {"Authorization": f"token {github_token}"} if github_token else {}
20 |     
21 |     api_resp = requests.get(
22 |         f"https://api.github.com/repos/{owner}/{name}",
23 |         headers=headers,
24 |         timeout=10
25 |     )
26 |     
27 |     if api_resp.status_code == 404:
28 |         raise ValueError(f"Repository '{owner}/{name}' not found or is private.")
29 |     elif api_resp.status_code == 403:
30 |         raise ValueError("GitHub API rate limit exceeded. Please try again in a minute or add a GITHUB_TOKEN to your .env file.")
31 |     elif api_resp.status_code != 200:
32 |         raise ValueError(f"GitHub API error: {api_resp.status_code}")
33 |     
34 |     repo_data = api_resp.json()
35 |     default_branch = repo_data.get("default_branch", "main")
36 |     
37 |     # Get language breakdown
38 |     lang_resp = requests.get(
39 |         f"https://api.github.com/repos/{owner}/{name}/languages",
40 |         headers=headers,
41 |         timeout=10
42 |     )
43 |     languages = lang_resp.json() if lang_resp.status_code == 200 else {}
44 |     
45 |     # Generate session and path
46 |     session_id = str(uuid.uuid4())
47 |     tmp_base = os.path.join(tempfile.gettempdir(), "repos")
48 |     os.makedirs(tmp_base, exist_ok=True)
49 |     repo_path = os.path.join(tmp_base, f"{owner}_{name}_{session_id}")
50 |     
51 |     # Clone repository — limit depth to 100 for speed; still enough for git history
52 |     cloned_repo = Repo.clone_from(repo_url, repo_path, depth=100)
53 |     commit_hash = cloned_repo.head.commit.hexsha
54 |     
55 |     set_session(session_id, {"repo_path": repo_path})
56 |     
57 |     # Quick count of files (skip .git)
58 |     total_files = sum(
59 |         len(files) for root, dirs, files in os.walk(repo_path)
60 |         if '.git' not in root
61 |     )
62 |     
63 |     return {
64 |         "session_id": session_id,
65 |         "repo": {
66 |             "owner": owner,
67 |             "name": name,
68 |             "branch": default_branch,
69 |             "commit_hash": commit_hash[:7],
70 |             "url": repo_url,
71 |             "total_files": total_files,
72 |             "languages": languages
73 |         },
74 |         "repo_path": repo_path
75 |     }
</content>
</file>
<file><path>codePulse-clean/frontend/src/services/api.ts</path>
<content lines="1-60">
 1 | const BASE_URL = import.meta.env.VITE_API_URL || "http://localhost:8000";
 2 | 
 3 | async function apiFetch(url: string, options?: RequestInit) {
 4 |   const res = await fetch(url, options);
 5 |   if (!res.ok) {
 6 |     // Try to extract a FastAPI error detail message
 7 |     let detail = `HTTP ${res.status}`;
 8 |     try {
 9 |       const body = await res.json();
10 |       detail = body.detail || detail;
11 |     } catch {}
12 |     throw new Error(detail);
13 |   }
14 |   return res.json();
15 | }
16 | 
17 | export async function scanRepo(repoUrl: string) {
18 |   return apiFetch(`${BASE_URL}/api/scan`, {
19 |     method: "POST",
20 |     headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" },
21 |     body: JSON.stringify({ repo_url: repoUrl }),
22 |   });
23 |   // Returns: { session_id, repo, file_tree, status }
24 | }
25 | 
26 | export async function getScanStatus(sessionId: string) {
27 |   return apiFetch(`${BASE_URL}/api/scan/${sessionId}/status`);
28 |   // Returns: { session_id, status, progress, ai_ready }
29 | }
30 | 
31 | export async function getAnalysis(sessionId: string) {
32 |   return apiFetch(`${BASE_URL}/api/analysis/${sessionId}`);
33 |   // Returns: { health_score, heatmap, dependency_graph, api_routes }
34 | }
35 | 
36 | export async function getGitHistory(sessionId: string) {
37 |   return apiFetch(`${BASE_URL}/api/analysis/${sessionId}/git-history`);
38 |   // Returns: { commits, commit_frequency, author_stats }
39 | }
40 | 
41 | export async function getRepoSummary(sessionId: string) {
42 |   return apiFetch(`${BASE_URL}/api/analysis/${sessionId}/summary`);
43 |   // Returns: { summary }
44 | }
45 | 
46 | export async function sendChat(sessionId: string, message: string, tab: string) {
47 |   return apiFetch(`${BASE_URL}/api/chat`, {
48 |     method: "POST",
49 |     headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" },
50 |     body: JSON.stringify({ session_id: sessionId, message, active_tab: tab }),
51 |   });
52 |   // Returns: { response, sources, model }
53 | }
54 | 
55 | export async function triggerEmbed(sessionId: string) {
56 |   return apiFetch(`${BASE_URL}/api/analysis/${sessionId}/embed`, {
57 |     method: "POST",
58 |   });
59 |   // Returns: { status, message }
60 | }
</content>

**Security Audit Complete - 18 Critical Issues Found**

Your project has **significant security vulnerabilities** that need immediate attention. All findings have been added to the Bob Findings panel.

## 🚨 CRITICAL Issues (Immediate Action Required):

1. **Exposed API Credentials** - WatsonX API key committed to repository
2. **Command Injection Risk** - Unsanitized git clone operations
3. **No Authentication** - All API endpoints publicly accessible

## ⚠️ HIGH Severity Issues:

- No input validation on repository URLs
- CORS misconfiguration with overly permissive settings
- Cloned repositories never cleaned up (disk exhaustion risk)
- Session management vulnerabilities
- Path traversal risks in file operations

## 📋 MEDIUM Severity Issues:

- Unpinned dependencies in requirements.txt
- Missing security headers (CSP, X-Frame-Options, etc.)
- No rate limiting (DoS vulnerability)
- Outdated jspdf dependency
- Information disclosure through detailed error messages
- No chat message length validation

## Immediate Actions Required:

1. **Revoke and regenerate** WatsonX API credentials immediately
2. Remove `.env` from git history using BFG Repo-Cleaner
3. Implement authentication/authorization
4. Add input validation and sanitization
5. Pin all dependency versions
6. Implement rate limiting and timeouts

Review all 18 issues in the Bob Findings panel for detailed remediation steps.

  1 | # CodePulse AI 🚀
  2 | 
  3 | > **Deep architectural intelligence that maps, analyzes, and decodes complex repositories instantly.**
  4 | 
  5 | CodePulse AI is an intelligent code analysis platform that helps developers understand any codebase in minutes. Using advanced AI-powered analysis, it provides comprehensive insights into code complexity, dependencies, git history, and architectural patterns.
  6 | 
  7 | [![License](https://img.shields.io/badge/license-MIT-blue.svg)](LICENSE)
  8 | [![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/python-3.9+-blue.svg)](https://www.python.org/downloads/)
  9 | [![React](https://img.shields.io/badge/react-18.3.1-blue.svg)](https://reactjs.org/)
 10 | [![FastAPI](https://img.shields.io/badge/fastapi-latest-green.svg)](https://fastapi.tiangolo.com/)
 11 | 
 12 | ---
 13 | 
 14 | ## 🎯 Features
 15 | 
 16 | ### 🔍 **Intelligent Code Analysis**
 17 | - **Complexity Heatmaps**: Visual representation of code complexity across your entire codebase
 18 | - **Risk Assessment**: Automated identification of high-risk files and potential bug hotspots
 19 | - **Dependency Graphs**: Interactive 3D visualization of module dependencies and relationships
 20 | - **Code Quality Metrics**: Comprehensive scoring based on complexity, duplication, and test coverage
 21 | 
 22 | ### 🤖 **AI-Powered Insights**
 23 | - **IBM WatsonX Integration**: Chat with your codebase using advanced AI
 24 | - **RAG (Retrieval-Augmented Generation)**: Context-aware responses based on your actual code
 25 | - **Smart Onboarding**: Automatically generated learning paths for new developers
 26 | - **Intelligent Suggestions**: AI-driven recommendations for code improvements
 27 | 
 28 | ### 📊 **Advanced Visualizations**
 29 | - **MRI Scan View**: Deep dive into code health with interactive treemaps
 30 | - **Git History Analysis**: Commit patterns, author statistics, and bug fix tracking
 31 | - **Scale Simulation**: Predict system behavior under load based on code metrics
 32 | - **API Route Mapping**: Automatic detection and visualization of API endpoints
 33 | 
 34 | ### 🎨 **Modern UI/UX**
 35 | - **3D Interactive Elements**: Spline-powered 3D visualizations
 36 | - **Responsive Design**: Works seamlessly on desktop and mobile
 37 | - **Dark/Light Themes**: Customizable interface preferences
 38 | - **Real-time Updates**: Live analysis as you explore your codebase
 39 | 
 40 | ---
 41 | 
 42 | ## 🏗️ Architecture
 43 | 
 44 | ```
 45 | codePulse-clean/
 46 | ├── frontend/                 # React + Vite frontend
 47 | │   ├── src/
 48 | │   │   ├── app/
 49 | │   │   │   ├── components/  # React components
 50 | │   │   │   │   ├── LandingPage.tsx
 51 | │   │   │   │   ├── ScanPage.tsx
 52 | │   │   │   │   ├── DashboardPage.tsx
 53 | │   │   │   │   └── RiskTreemapVisualization.tsx
 54 | │   │   │   ├── App.tsx
 55 | │   │   │   └── routes.tsx
 56 | │   │   ├── services/        # API integration
 57 | │   │   └── styles/          # Global styles
 58 | │   └── vite.config.ts
 59 | │
 60 | └── backend/                  # FastAPI backend
 61 |     ├── routes/              # API endpoints
 62 |     │   ├── scan.py          # Repository scanning
 63 |     │   ├── analysis.py      # Code analysis
 64 |     │   └── chat.py          # AI chat interface
 65 |     ├── services/            # Core business logic
 66 |     │   ├── repo_ingestion.py    # GitHub repo cloning
 67 |     │   ├── code_parser.py       # AST parsing
 68 |     │   ├── complexity.py        # Complexity analysis
 69 |     │   ├── dependency_graph.py  # Dependency mapping
 70 |     │   ├── embeddings.py        # Vector embeddings (RAG)
 71 |     │   ├── watsonx_client.py    # IBM WatsonX AI
 72 |     │   ├── git_history.py       # Git analysis
 73 |     │   ├── onboarding.py        # Learning path generation
 74 |     │   └── scale_simulator.py   # Load prediction
 75 |     ├── models/              # Pydantic models
 76 |     ├── utils/               # Utilities
 77 |     └── tests/               # Unit tests
 78 | ```
 79 | 
 80 | ### Technology Stack
 81 | 
 82 | **Frontend:**
 83 | - React 18.3.1 + TypeScript
 84 | - Vite 6.4.2 (build tool)
 85 | - TailwindCSS 4.1.12 (styling)
 86 | - shadcn/ui (component library)
 87 | - Recharts (data visualization)
 88 | - React Router 7.13.0 (routing)
 89 | - Spline (3D graphics)
 90 | - Motion (animations)
 91 | 
 92 | **Backend:**
 93 | - FastAPI (Python web framework)
 94 | - GitPython (git operations)
 95 | - ChromaDB (vector database for RAG)
 96 | - Sentence Transformers (embeddings)
 97 | - IBM WatsonX AI (LLM integration)
 98 | - NetworkX (graph analysis)
 99 | - Radon & Lizard (code metrics)
100 | 
101 | ---
102 | 
103 | ## 🚀 Quick Start
104 | 
105 | ### Prerequisites
106 | 
107 | - **Node.js** 18+ and npm
108 | - **Python** 3.9+
109 | - **Git**
110 | - **IBM WatsonX API Key** (for AI features)
111 | 
112 | ### Installation
113 | 
114 | #### 1. Clone the Repository
115 | 
116 | ```bash
117 | git clone https://github.com/yourusername/codepulse-ai.git
118 | cd codepulse-ai/codePulse-clean
119 | ```
120 | 
121 | #### 2. Backend Setup
122 | 
123 | ```bash
124 | cd backend
125 | 
126 | # Create virtual environment
127 | python -m venv venv
128 | source venv/bin/activate  # On Windows: venv\Scripts\activate
129 | 
130 | # Install dependencies
131 | pip install -r requirements.txt
132 | 
133 | # Configure environment variables
134 | cp .env.example .env
135 | # Edit .env and add your credentials:
136 | # WATSONX_API_KEY=your_api_key_here
137 | # WATSONX_PROJECT_ID=your_project_id_here
138 | # WATSONX_REGION=us-south
139 | # GITHUB_TOKEN=your_github_token_here (optional)
140 | ```
141 | 
142 | #### 3. Frontend Setup
143 | 
144 | ```bash
145 | cd ../frontend
146 | 
147 | # Install dependencies
148 | npm install
149 | 
150 | # Configure environment variables (optional)
151 | cp .env.example .env
152 | # Edit .env if needed:
153 | # VITE_API_URL=http://localhost:8000
154 | ```
155 | 
156 | ### Running the Application
157 | 
158 | #### Start Backend Server
159 | 
160 | ```bash
161 | cd codePulse-clean/backend
162 | uvicorn main:app --reload --port 8000
163 | ```
164 | 
165 | Backend will be available at: `http://localhost:8000`
166 | 
167 | #### Start Frontend Development Server
168 | 
169 | ```bash
170 | cd codePulse-clean/frontend
171 | npm run dev
172 | ```
173 | 
174 | Frontend will be available at: `http://localhost:5173`
175 | 
176 | ### First Analysis
177 | 
178 | 1. Open `http://localhost:5173` in your browser
179 | 2. Enter a GitHub repository URL (e.g., `https://github.com/facebook/react`)
180 | 3. Click "Scan Repository"
181 | 4. Wait for analysis to complete
182 | 5. Explore the interactive dashboard!
183 | 
184 | ---
185 | 
186 | ## 📖 Usage Guide
187 | 
188 | ### Scanning a Repository
189 | 
190 | 1. **Navigate to Scan Page**: Click "Get Started" or navigate to `/scan`
191 | 2. **Enter Repository URL**: Paste any public GitHub repository URL
192 | 3. **Start Scan**: Click "Scan Repository" button
193 | 4. **Wait for Analysis**: The system will:
194 |    - Clone the repository (shallow clone, depth=100)
195 |    - Parse all source files
196 |    - Calculate complexity metrics
197 |    - Build dependency graphs
198 |    - Generate embeddings for AI chat
199 |    - Analyze git history
200 | 
201 | ### Understanding the Dashboard
202 | 
203 | #### **MRI Scan Tab**
204 | - **Health Score**: Overall code quality (0-100)
205 | - **Risk Treemap**: Interactive visualization of file-level risk
206 | - **Complexity Distribution**: See which files need attention
207 | - **Color Coding**:
208 |   - 🟢 Green: Low risk (< 30)
209 |   - 🟡 Yellow: Medium risk (30-65)
210 |   - 🔴 Red: High risk (> 65)
211 | 
212 | #### **Heatmap Tab**
213 | - Detailed file-by-file complexity analysis
214 | - Sort by risk, complexity, or lines of code
215 | - Click files to see detailed metrics
216 | 
217 | #### **Dependencies Tab**
218 | - Interactive 3D force-directed graph
219 | - Node colors represent component types:
220 |   - 🔵 Blue: API/Routes
221 |   - 🟢 Green: Components
222 |   - 🟡 Yellow: Services
223 |   - 🔴 Red: Database/Models
224 |   - ⚪ Gray: Utilities
225 | 
226 | #### **Git History Tab**
227 | - Commit frequency over time
228 | - Author contribution statistics
229 | - Bug fix patterns
230 | - Code churn analysis
231 | 
232 | #### **Scale Simulation Tab**
233 | - Predicted system behavior under load
234 | - Bottleneck identification
235 | - Latency projections
236 | - Component failure analysis
237 | 
238 | ### AI Chat Features
239 | 
240 | The AI assistant can help you:
241 | - Understand complex code patterns
242 | - Get onboarding guidance
243 | - Find specific implementations
244 | - Explain architectural decisions
245 | - Suggest improvements
246 | 
247 | **Example Questions:**
248 | - "What does the authentication flow look like?"
249 | - "Which files handle database operations?"
250 | - "Explain the main entry point of this application"
251 | - "What are the high-risk areas I should focus on?"
252 | 
253 | ---
254 | 
255 | ## 🧪 Testing
256 | 
257 | ### Running Tests
258 | 
259 | ```bash
260 | cd codePulse-clean/backend
261 | 
262 | # Run all tests
263 | pytest
264 | 
265 | # Run with coverage
266 | pytest --cov=services --cov-report=html
267 | 
268 | # Run specific test file
269 | pytest tests/test_repo_ingestion.py
270 | 
271 | # Run with verbose output
272 | pytest -v
273 | ```
274 | 
275 | ### Test Coverage
276 | 
277 | Current test coverage:
278 | - `services/repo_ingestion.py`: ~95%
279 | - More tests coming soon!
280 | 
281 | See [backend/README_TESTS.md](backend/README_TESTS.md) for detailed testing guide.
282 |

284 | 
285 | ## 🔧 Configuration
286 | 
287 | ### Backend Environment Variables
288 | 
289 | Create `backend/.env` file:
290 | 
291 | ```env
292 | # IBM WatsonX AI Configuration
293 | WATSONX_API_KEY=your_api_key_here
294 | WATSONX_PROJECT_ID=your_project_id_here
295 | WATSONX_REGION=us-south
296 | WATSONX_MODEL_ID=meta-llama/llama-3-3-70b-instruct
297 | 
298 | # GitHub API (optional, for higher rate limits)
299 | GITHUB_TOKEN=your_github_token_here
300 | ```
301 | 
302 | ### Frontend Environment Variables
303 | 
304 | Create `frontend/.env` file:
305 | 
306 | ```env
307 | # API Base URL (optional, defaults to http://localhost:8000)
308 | VITE_API_URL=http://localhost:8000
309 | ```
310 | 
311 | ### Mock Mode
312 | 
313 | If you don't have WatsonX credentials, the system will automatically fall back to mock mode:
314 | - Set `WATSONX_API_KEY=your_api_key_here` (or leave unset)
315 | - AI chat will return mock responses
316 | - All other features work normally

385 | ## 🐛 Troubleshooting
386 | 
387 | ### Common Issues
388 | 
389 | **Backend won't start:**
390 | - Ensure Python 3.9+ is installed
391 | - Check that all dependencies are installed: `pip install -r requirements.txt`
392 | - Verify `.env` file exists in `backend/` directory
393 | 
394 | **Frontend won't start:**
395 | - Ensure Node.js 18+ is installed
396 | - Delete `node_modules` and run `npm install` again
397 | - Check for port conflicts (default: 5173)
398 | 
399 | **AI chat not working:**
400 | - Verify WatsonX credentials in `.env`
401 | - Check backend logs for authentication errors
402 | - Ensure embeddings were built (POST to `/api/analysis/{session_id}/embed`)
403 | 
404 | **Repository scan fails:**
405 | - Check GitHub API rate limits
406 | - Add `GITHUB_TOKEN` to `.env` for higher limits
407 | - Ensure repository is public or you have access

## 🏗️ CodePulse AI - Overall Architecture

**CodePulse AI** is an intelligent code analysis platform that helps developers understand complex codebases through AI-powered insights, visualizations, and metrics.

### High-Level Architecture

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    FRONTEND (React + Vite)                   │
│  ┌──────────────┐  ┌──────────────┐  ┌──────────────┐      │
│  │ LandingPage  │→ │  ScanPage    │→ │ DashboardPage│      │
│  └──────────────┘  └──────────────┘  └──────────────┘      │
│         │                  │                  │              │
│         └──────────────────┴──────────────────┘              │
│                            │                                 │
│                    ┌───────▼────────┐                        │
│                    │  API Service   │                        │
│                    │  (api.ts)      │                        │
└────────────────────┴────────────────┴────────────────────────┘
                             │
                    HTTP/REST API
                             │
┌────────────────────────────▼─────────────────────────────────┐
│                  BACKEND (FastAPI)                            │
│  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │              API Routes Layer                         │    │
│  │  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐          │    │
│  │  │ scan.py  │  │analysis.py│  │ chat.py  │          │    │
│  │  └────┬─────┘  └─────┬─────┘  └────┬─────┘          │    │
│  └───────┼──────────────┼─────────────┼────────────────┘    │
│          │              │             │                      │
│  ┌───────▼──────────────▼─────────────▼────────────────┐    │
│  │              Services Layer                          │    │
│  │  ┌─────────────────┐  ┌──────────────────┐         │    │
│  │  │ repo_ingestion  │  │  code_parser     │         │    │
│  │  │ (Git Clone)     │  │  (AST Analysis)  │         │    │
│  │  └─────────────────┘  └──────────────────┘         │    │
│  │  ┌─────────────────┐  ┌──────────────────┐         │    │
│  │  │  complexity     │  │ dependency_graph │         │    │
│  │  │  (Metrics)      │  │  (NetworkX)      │         │    │
│  │  └─────────────────┘  └──────────────────┘         │    │
│  │  ┌─────────────────┐  ┌──────────────────┐         │    │
│  │  │  embeddings     │  │  watsonx_client  │         │    │
│  │  │  (ChromaDB+RAG) │  │  (IBM AI)        │         │    │
│  │  └─────────────────┘  └──────────────────┘         │    │
│  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
└───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                             │
                    ┌────────▼────────┐
                    │  External APIs  │
                    │  - GitHub API   │
                    │  - WatsonX AI   │
                    └─────────────────┘
```

### Component Interaction Flow

#### 1️⃣ **Repository Scanning Flow**
```
User enters GitHub URL → ScanPage → POST /api/scan
                                         ↓
                              repo_ingestion.py
                              - Validates URL
                              - Clones repo (depth=100)
                              - Generates session_id
                                         ↓
                              code_parser.py
                              - Parses file tree
                              - Extracts API routes
                                         ↓
                              Background: embeddings.py
                              - Chunks code files
                              - Generates vectors
                              - Stores in ChromaDB
                                         ↓
                              Returns: session_id + metadata
```

#### 2️⃣ **Analysis Flow**
```
User views Dashboard → GET /api/analysis/{session_id}
                                         ↓
                       ┌─────────────────┴─────────────────┐
                       │                                   │
              complexity.py                    dependency_graph.py
              - Calculates metrics             - Builds module graph
              - Generates heatmap              - Identifies relationships
              - Risk assessment                - 3D visualization data
                       │                                   │
                       └─────────────────┬─────────────────┘
                                         ↓
                              git_history.py
                              - Commit analysis
                              - Author stats
                              - Bug patterns
                                         ↓
                              scale_simulator.py
                              - Load predictions
                              - Bottleneck detection
                                         ↓
                       Returns: Complete analysis data
```

#### 3️⃣ **AI Chat Flow (RAG)**
```
User asks question → POST /api/chat
                              ↓
                    embeddings.py (retrieve_context)
                    - Converts query to vector
                    - Searches ChromaDB
                    - Retrieves relevant code chunks
                              ↓
                    watsonx_client.py
                    - Builds prompt with context
                    - Calls IBM WatsonX API
                    - Returns AI response
                              ↓
                    Returns: Answer + source files
```

### Key Technologies & Their Roles

**Frontend Stack:**
- **React 18.3.1**: UI framework
- **Vite**: Fast build tool & dev server
- **TailwindCSS**: Utility-first styling
- **Recharts**: Data visualization charts
- **React Router 7**: Client-side routing
- **Spline**: 3D interactive graphics

**Backend Stack:**
- **FastAPI**: High-performance Python web framework
- **GitPython**: Git repository operations
- **ChromaDB**: Vector database for embeddings
- **Sentence Transformers**: Text-to-vector conversion
- **IBM WatsonX**: LLM for AI chat (Llama 3.3 70B)
- **NetworkX**: Graph analysis for dependencies
- **Radon & Lizard**: Code complexity metrics

### Data Flow & Storage

**Session Management:**
- In-memory cache stores session data
- Session ID (UUID) links user to cloned repo
- No persistence - data lost on restart

**Repository Storage:**
- Cloned to `/tmp/repos/{owner}_{name}_{session_id}`
- Shallow clone (depth=100) for performance
- ⚠️ **Security Issue**: Never cleaned up

**Vector Embeddings:**
- Code chunked into 50-line segments (10-line overlap)
- Stored in ChromaDB collection per session
- Used for RAG-based AI responses

### Security Architecture (Current State)

**⚠️ Critical Gaps Identified:**
1. **No Authentication**: All endpoints publicly accessible
2. **No Authorization**: No user/session validation
3. **No Rate Limiting**: Vulnerable to DoS attacks
4. **Exposed Credentials**: API keys in committed `.env`
5. **No Input Validation**: URLs, messages not sanitized
6. **No Session Expiry**: Memory leaks from accumulating sessions

### Recommended Security Improvements

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Add Authentication Layer (JWT/OAuth2)              │
│  ↓                                                   │
│  Add Rate Limiting Middleware (slowapi)             │
│  ↓                                                   │
│  Add Input Validation (Pydantic + custom validators)│
│  ↓                                                   │
│  Add Security Headers (CSP, X-Frame-Options, etc.)  │
│  ↓                                                   │
│  Implement Session Management (Redis with TTL)      │
│  ↓                                                   │
│  Add Resource Cleanup (scheduled jobs)              │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Summary**: CodePulse AI is a well-architected application with clean separation of concerns, but requires immediate security hardening before production deployment. The 18 security issues identified in the Bob Findings panel must be addressed, particularly the exposed credentials and lack of authentication.

## 🔄 Complete Data Flow: User Input → Final Output

### **Scenario 1: Repository Scanning & Analysis**

#### **Step 1: User Enters Repository URL**
```
User on LandingPage → Clicks "Get Started"
                    ↓
              Navigates to /scan
                    ↓
         Enters: "https://github.com/facebook/react"
                    ↓
              Clicks "Scan Repository"
```

**Frontend Code Path:**
- [`ScanPage.tsx`](codePulse-clean/frontend/src/app/components/ScanPage.tsx) captures input
- Calls [`scanRepo(repoUrl)`](codePulse-clean/frontend/src/services/api.ts:17) from api.ts
- Makes POST request to `http://localhost:8000/api/scan`

---

#### **Step 2: Backend Receives Request**
```
POST /api/scan
Body: { "repo_url": "https://github.com/facebook/react" }
                    ↓
         FastAPI routes to scan.py
```

**Backend Entry Point:**
- [`main.py`](codePulse-clean/backend/main.py:24) includes scan router
- [`scan.py:scan_repository()`](codePulse-clean/backend/routes/scan.py:20) handles request

---

#### **Step 3: Repository Ingestion**
```
scan_repository() calls ingest_repository(repo_url)
                    ↓
         repo_ingestion.py processes
```

**Detailed Flow in [`repo_ingestion.py`](codePulse-clean/backend/services/repo_ingestion.py):**

1. **Parse URL** (line 8-12):
   ```python
   owner, name = parse_github_url(url)
   # Result: owner="facebook", name="react"
   ```

2. **Fetch Metadata** (line 21-34):
   ```python
   # Calls GitHub API
   GET https://api.github.com/repos/facebook/react
   # Returns: stars, forks, default_branch, etc.
   ```

3. **Get Languages** (line 38-43):
   ```python
   GET https://api.github.com/repos/facebook/react/languages
   # Returns: {"JavaScript": 1234567, "TypeScript": 987654, ...}
   ```

4. **Generate Session** (line 46):
   ```python
   session_id = str(uuid.uuid4())
   # Result: "a1b2c3d4-e5f6-7890-abcd-ef1234567890"
   ```

5. **Clone Repository** (line 52):
   ```python
   Repo.clone_from(repo_url, repo_path, depth=100)
   # Clones to: /tmp/repos/facebook_react_a1b2c3d4...
   # Shallow clone: only last 100 commits
   ```

6. **Store Session** (line 55):
   ```python
   set_session(session_id, {"repo_path": repo_path})
   # Stores in memory cache
   ```

**Output:**
```json
{
  "session_id": "a1b2c3d4-e5f6-7890-abcd-ef1234567890",
  "repo": {
    "owner": "facebook",
    "name": "react",
    "branch": "main",
    "commit_hash": "abc1234",
    "url": "https://github.com/facebook/react",
    "total_files": 1523,
    "languages": {"JavaScript": 60%, "TypeScript": 35%, ...}
  },
  "repo_path": "/tmp/repos/facebook_react_a1b2c3d4..."
}
```

---

#### **Step 4: Code Parsing**
```
scan_repository() calls parse_codebase(repo_path)
                    ↓
         code_parser.py analyzes files
```

**Detailed Flow in [`code_parser.py`](codePulse-clean/backend/services/code_parser.py):**

1. **Walk Directory Tree**:
   ```python
   for root, dirs, files in os.walk(repo_path):
       # Skips: node_modules, .git, __pycache__, etc.
   ```

2. **Parse Each File**:
   ```python
   # For Python files: uses ast.parse()
   # For JS/TS files: regex pattern matching
   # Extracts: functions, classes, imports, API routes
   ```

3. **Build File Tree**:
   ```json
   {
     "name": "react",
     "type": "folder",
     "children": [
       {
         "name": "src",
         "type": "folder",
         "children": [
           {
             "name": "index.js",
             "type": "file",
             "size": 1234,
             "lines": 45,
             "functions": ["render", "createElement"],
             "api_routes": []
           }
         ]
       }
     ]
   }
   ```

---

#### **Step 5: Background Embedding Generation**
```
BackgroundTasks.add_task(_embed_in_background)
                    ↓
    Runs asynchronously while response returns
```

**Detailed Flow in [`embeddings.py`](codePulse-clean/backend/services/embeddings.py):**

1. **Chunk Code Files**:
   ```python
   CHUNK_SIZE = 50 lines
   OVERLAP = 10 lines
   
   # Example: 200-line file becomes 4 chunks:
   # Chunk 1: lines 1-50
   # Chunk 2: lines 41-90 (10-line overlap)
   # Chunk 3: lines 81-130
   # Chunk 4: lines 121-170
   ```

2. **Generate Embeddings**:
   ```python
   model = SentenceTransformer('all-MiniLM-L6-v2')
   embeddings = model.encode(chunks)
   # Converts text → 384-dimensional vectors
   ```

3. **Store in ChromaDB**:
   ```python
   collection = chroma_client.get_or_create_collection(session_id)
   collection.add(
       documents=chunks,
       embeddings=embeddings,
       metadatas=[{"file": "src/index.js", "line": 1}]
   )
   ```

---

#### **Step 6: Response Returns to Frontend**
```
Backend sends response → Frontend receives
                    ↓
         ScanPage updates state
                    ↓
    Navigates to /dashboard?session={session_id}
```

**Frontend receives:**
```json
{
  "session_id": "a1b2c3d4...",
  "repo": {...},
  "file_tree": {...},
  "status": "scan_complete"
}
```

---

### **Scenario 2: Dashboard Analysis**

#### **Step 7: Dashboard Loads**
```
User on /dashboard?session=a1b2c3d4...
                    ↓
    DashboardPage.tsx componentDidMount()
                    ↓
    Calls getAnalysis(sessionId)
```

**Frontend makes request:**
```
GET /api/analysis/a1b2c3d4-e5f6-7890-abcd-ef1234567890
```

---

#### **Step 8: Backend Generates Analysis**
```
analysis.py:get_analysis() processes
```

**Detailed Flow in [`analysis.py`](codePulse-clean/backend/routes/analysis.py:31):**

1. **Retrieve Session** (line 33):
   ```python
   session_data = get_session(session_id)
   repo_path = session_data["repo_path"]
   ```

2. **Re-parse Codebase** (line 39):
   ```python
   file_tree = parse_codebase(repo_path)
   ```

3. **Calculate Complexity** (line 42):
   ```python
   # complexity.py uses Radon & Lizard
   heatmap_files = calculate_heatmap(file_tree)
   # Returns: [{name: "index.js", complexity: 45, risk: 65}, ...]
   ```

4. **Build Dependency Graph** (line 44):
   ```python
   # dependency_graph.py uses NetworkX
   dependency_graph = build_dependency_graph(file_tree)
   # Returns: {nodes: [...], links: [...]}
   ```

5. **Extract API Routes** (line 45):
   ```python
   api_routes = extract_all_routes(file_tree)
   # Returns: [{method: "GET", path: "/api/users", file: "routes.js"}]
   ```

6. **Generate Onboarding Path** (line 46):
   ```python
   # onboarding.py creates learning path
   onboarding_path, questions = generate_onboarding_data(file_tree)
   ```

7. **Calculate Health Score** (line 48-50):
   ```python
   avg_complexity = sum(complexities) / total_files
   health_score = max(0, 100 - (avg_complexity // 2))
   # Example: avg_complexity=40 → health_score=80
   ```

8. **Simulate Scale** (line 78-81):
   ```python
   # scale_simulator.py predicts performance
   scale_profile = compute_scale_profile(...)
   # Returns: latency predictions, bottlenecks, etc.
   ```

**Response sent to frontend:**
```json
{
  "health_score": 80,
  "heatmap_files": [...],
  "heatmap_tree": {...},
  "directory_tree": {...},
  "languages": {"JavaScript": 60, "TypeScript": 35},
  "dependency_graph": {
    "nodes": [...],
    "links": [...]
  },
  "api_routes": [...],
  "total_files": 1523,
  "avg_complexity": 40,
  "onboarding_path": [...],
  "suggested_questions": [...],
  "scale_profile": {...}
}
```

---

#### **Step 9: Frontend Renders Visualizations**
```
DashboardPage receives analysis data
                    ↓
    Updates state with all metrics
                    ↓
    Renders tabs: MRI Scan, Heatmap, Dependencies, etc.
```

**Visualization Components:**
- **MRI Scan Tab**: [`RiskTreemapVisualization.tsx`](codePulse-clean/frontend/src/app/components/RiskTreemapVisualization.tsx) renders treemap
- **Heatmap Tab**: Recharts displays complexity bars
- **Dependencies Tab**: react-force-graph-3d renders 3D graph
- **Git History Tab**: Fetches from `/api/analysis/{session_id}/git-history`

---

### **Scenario 3: AI Chat Interaction**

#### **Step 10: User Asks Question**
```
User types: "What does the authentication flow look like?"
                    ↓
         Clicks Send in chat interface
                    ↓
    Calls sendChat(sessionId, message, activeTab)
```

**Frontend makes request:**
```
POST /api/chat
Body: {
  "session_id": "a1b2c3d4...",
  "message": "What does the authentication flow look like?",
  "active_tab": "dependencies"
}
```

---

#### **Step 11: RAG Context Retrieval**
```
chat.py:handle_chat() processes
                    ↓
    retrieve_context(session_id, message)
```

**Detailed Flow in [`embeddings.py`](codePulse-clean/backend/services/embeddings.py):**

1. **Convert Query to Vector**:
   ```python
   query_embedding = model.encode(message)
   # "What does the authentication flow look like?"
   # → [0.123, -0.456, 0.789, ...] (384 dimensions)
   ```

2. **Search ChromaDB**:
   ```python
   collection = chroma_client.get_collection(session_id)
   results = collection.query(
       query_embeddings=[query_embedding],
       n_results=5  # Top 5 most relevant chunks
   )
   ```

3. **Retrieve Relevant Code**:
   ```python
   # Returns chunks like:
   sources = [
       {"file": "auth/login.js", "line": 15},
       {"file": "middleware/auth.js", "line": 8},
       ...
   ]
   context = """
   // auth/login.js:15
   function authenticateUser(username, password) {
       const token = jwt.sign({user: username}, SECRET);
       return token;
   }
   
   // middleware/auth.js:8
   function verifyToken(req, res, next) {
       const token = req.headers.authorization;
       ...
   }
   """
   ```

---

#### **Step 12: AI Response Generation**
```
chat_with_granite(prompt, context)
                    ↓
         watsonx_client.py calls IBM WatsonX
```

**Detailed Flow in [`watsonx_client.py`](codePulse-clean/backend/services/watsonx_client.py):**

1. **Build Prompt**:
   ```python
   full_prompt = f"""
   You are a code analysis assistant. Use the following code context:
   
   {context}
   
   User question: {message}
   
   Provide a clear, technical explanation.
   """
   ```

2. **Call WatsonX API**:
   ```python
   response = requests.post(
       f"https://{region}.ml.cloud.ibm.com/ml/v1/text/generation",
       headers={"Authorization": f"Bearer {api_key}"},
       json={
           "model_id": "meta-llama/llama-3-3-70b-instruct",
           "input": full_prompt,
           "parameters": {
               "max_new_tokens": 500,
               "temperature": 0.7
           }
       }
   )
   ```

3. **Extract Response**:
   ```python
   ai_response = response.json()["results"][0]["generated_text"]
   ```

---

#### **Step 13: Response Returns to User**
```
Backend sends ChatResponse → Frontend displays
```

**User sees:**
```
AI Assistant:
The authentication flow in this repository follows a JWT-based approach:

1. User Login (auth/login.js:15):
   - User submits credentials
   - Server validates against database
   - JWT token generated with user info

2. Token Verification (middleware/auth.js:8):
   - Each protected route checks Authorization header
   - Token decoded and validated
   - User info attached to request

3. Key Files:
   - auth/login.js: Handles login logic
   - middleware/auth.js: Token verification
   - config/jwt.js: JWT configuration

Sources: auth/login.js:15, middleware/auth.js:8
```

---

## 📊 Data Flow Summary

```
USER INPUT (GitHub URL)
    ↓
FRONTEND (React)
    ↓ HTTP POST
BACKEND API (FastAPI)
    ↓
REPO INGESTION (GitPython)
    ↓ Clone & Parse
CODE ANALYSIS (Radon, Lizard, NetworkX)
    ↓ Generate Metrics
EMBEDDINGS (ChromaDB + Sentence Transformers)
    ↓ Store Vectors
RESPONSE TO FRONTEND
    ↓
VISUALIZATIONS (Recharts, 3D Graphs)
    ↓
USER SEES DASHBOARD
    ↓
USER ASKS AI QUESTION
    ↓
RAG RETRIEVAL (ChromaDB Search)
    ↓
AI GENERATION (IBM WatsonX)
    ↓
RESPONSE TO USER
```

**Key Insight**: The entire system is stateless except for the in-memory session cache and ChromaDB collections. Each request independently fetches data from the cloned repository, making it scalable but also vulnerable to the security issues identified earlier.

 10 | def compute_scale_profile(heatmap_files, api_routes, dependency_graph, total_files, avg_complexity, languages):
 11 |     """
 12 |     Compute a scale simulation profile from real codebase analysis data.
 13 |     Returns a dict describing how the system would behave under increasing load.
 14 |     """
 15 |     
 16 |     # --- Derive base system characteristics from code metrics ---
 17 |     
 18 |     num_api_routes = len(api_routes) if api_routes else 0
 19 |     num_deps = len(dependency_graph.get("links", [])) if dependency_graph else 0
 20 |     num_nodes = len(dependency_graph.get("nodes", [])) if dependency_graph else 0
 21 |     
 22 |     # Calculate high-risk file count and average risk
 23 |     high_risk_files = [f for f in heatmap_files if f.get("risk", 0) >= 60]
 24 |     critical_files = [f for f in heatmap_files if f.get("risk", 0) >= 80]
 25 |     avg_risk = sum(f.get("risk", 0) for f in heatmap_files) / max(len(heatmap_files), 1)
 26 |     max_risk = max((f.get("risk", 0) for f in heatmap_files), default=0)
 27 |     
 28 |     # Coupling ratio: how interconnected the codebase is
 29 |     coupling_ratio = num_deps / max(num_nodes, 1)
 30 |     
 31 |     # Detect architectural components from file paths and API routes
 32 |     components = _detect_components(heatmap_files, api_routes)
 33 |     
 34 |     # --- Compute base latency (derived from code complexity) ---
 35 |     # Higher complexity = more processing per request = higher base latency
 36 |     base_latency = max(15, min(200, int(
 37 |         20 +                                    # minimum network overhead
 38 |         avg_complexity * 0.8 +                  # complexity overhead
 39 |         num_api_routes * 0.5 +                  # route matching overhead
 40 |         coupling_ratio * 5 +                    # dependency resolution overhead
 41 |         len(high_risk_files) * 0.3              # tech debt drag
 42 |     )))
 43 |     
 44 |     # --- Compute base error rate (derived from risk profile) ---
 45 |     base_error_rate = max(0.01, min(5.0, round(
 46 |         0.01 +                                   # baseline
 47 |         (avg_risk / 100) * 0.5 +                 # risk-proportional errors
 48 |         len(critical_files) * 0.05 +             # critical file hotspots
 49 |         (1 if avg_complexity > 60 else 0) * 0.3  # complexity penalty
 50 |     , 2)))
 51 |     
 52 |     # --- Compute base memory usage % (derived from codebase size) ---
 53 |     base_memory = max(10, min(50, int(
 54 |         15 +                                     # base runtime
 55 |         total_files * 0.05 +                     # per-file module load
 56 |         num_deps * 0.15 +                        # dependency memory

 58 |     )))
 59 |     
 60 |     # --- Compute component failure thresholds ---
 61 |     # Weakest components fail first. Thresholds based on complexity and coupling.

 69 |             "avgComplexity": comp["avgComplexity"],

 72 |             "healthyDetail": comp["healthyDetail"],
 73 |             "failingDetail": comp["failingDetail"],
 74 |         })
 75 |     
 76 |     # Sort by failure threshold (weakest first)
 77 |     component_profiles.sort(key=lambda c: c["failureThreshold"])
 78 |     
 79 |     # --- Compute scaling curve parameters ---

 81 |     latency_growth_rate = max(3, min(20, int(
 82 |         5 + avg_complexity * 0.15 + coupling_ratio * 2
 83 |     )))
 84 |

 87 |     , 3)))

 91 |     , 2)))
 92 |     
 93 |     # --- Generate AI recommendations based on actual analysis ---
 94 |     recommendations = _generate_recommendations(

 96 |         avg_risk, num_api_routes, coupling_ratio, total_files, languages
 97 |     )

100 |         "baseLatency": base_latency,
101 |         "baseErrorRate": base_error_rate,
102 |         "baseMemory": base_memory,

121 | def _detect_components(heatmap_files, api_routes):
122 |     """
123 |     Detect architectural components from the codebase file structure.
124 |     Groups files by their parent directory/module and classifies them.
125 |     """
126 |     # Group files by their top-level directory
127 |     dir_groups = {}
128 |     for f in heatmap_files:
129 |         parts = f.get("path", "").strip("/").split("/")
130 |         if len(parts) > 1:
131 |             top_dir = parts[0]
132 |         else:
133 |             top_dir = "root"
134 |         
135 |         if top_dir not in dir_groups:
136 |             dir_groups[top_dir] = []
137 |         dir_groups[top_dir].append(f)
138 |     
139 |     # Classify each group into a component type
140 |     components = []
141 |     for dir_name, files in dir_groups.items():
142 |         avg_comp = sum(f.get("complexity", 0) for f in files) / max(len(files), 1)
143 |         avg_risk_val = sum(f.get("risk", 0) for f in files) / max(len(files), 1)
144 |         
145 |         comp_type, healthy_detail, failing_detail = _classify_component(
146 |             dir_name, files, api_routes
147 |         )
148 |         
149 |         components.append({
150 |             "name": _format_component_name(dir_name, comp_type),
151 |             "type": comp_type,
152 |             "dirName": dir_name,
153 |             "fileCount": len(files),
154 |             "avgComplexity": round(avg_comp, 1),
155 |             "avgRisk": round(avg_risk_val, 1),
156 |             "healthyDetail": healthy_detail,
157 |             "failingDetail": failing_detail,
158 |         })
159 |     
160 |     return components

254 | def _compute_failure_threshold(component, coupling_ratio, avg_risk):
255 |     """
256 |     Compute the traffic multiplier at which this component fails.
257 |     Lower complexity and risk = higher threshold (more resilient).
258 |     """
259 |     base = 90  # maximum possible threshold
260 |     
261 |     # Complexity penalty: -0.3x per complexity point
262 |     complexity_penalty = component["avgComplexity"] * 0.3
263 |     
264 |     # Risk penalty: -0.2x per risk point
265 |     risk_penalty = component["avgRisk"] * 0.2
266 |     
267 |     # File count penalty: more files = more surface area for failure
268 |     file_penalty = min(15, component["fileCount"] * 0.5)
269 |     
270 |     # Coupling penalty
271 |     coupling_penalty = coupling_ratio * 3
272 |     
273 |     # Component type bonus: some types are inherently more resilient
274 |     type_bonuses = {
275 |         "static_assets": 30,
276 |         "config": 25,
277 |         "testing": 20,
278 |         "utilities": 10,
279 |         "frontend": 5,
280 |         "middleware": 0,
281 |         "business_logic": -5,
282 |         "api_gateway": -5,
283 |         "auth_service": -8,
284 |         "database": -10,
285 |         "module": 0,
286 |     }
287 |     type_bonus = type_bonuses.get(component["type"], 0)
288 |     
289 |     threshold = base - complexity_penalty - risk_penalty - file_penalty - coupling_penalty + type_bonus
290 |     return max(5, min(95, round(threshold)))
291 |

304 |                           f"It has {weakest['fileCount']} files with avg complexity {weakest['avgComplexity']}. "
305 |                           f"Reduce complexity to increase resilience.",
306 |             "impact": f"Increases failure threshold from {weakest['failureThreshold']}x to ~{min(95, weakest['failureThreshold'] + 20)}x",

122 | def parse_codebase(repo_path: str):
123 |     skip_dirs = {'node_modules', '.git', '__pycache__', 'dist', 'build', '.next', 'venv', '.venv', 'env'}
124 |     file_tree = {"name": "root", "type": "folder", "children": []}
125 |     
126 |     def walk_dir(current_path, current_node):
127 |         try:
128 |             entries = os.listdir(current_path)
129 |         except PermissionError:
130 |             return
131 |             
132 |         for entry in sorted(entries):
133 |             full_path = os.path.join(current_path, entry)
134 |             if os.path.isdir(full_path):
135 |                 if entry in skip_dirs:
136 |                     continue
137 |                 folder_node = {"name": entry, "type": "folder", "children": []}
138 |                 current_node["children"].append(folder_node)
139 |                 walk_dir(full_path, folder_node)
140 |             else:
141 |                 ext = os.path.splitext(entry)[1].lower()
142 |                 size = os.path.getsize(full_path)
143 |                 try:
144 |                     with open(full_path, 'r', encoding='utf-8') as f:
145 |                         content = f.read()
146 |                     lines = len(content.splitlines())
147 |                     
148 |                     file_info = {
149 |                         "name": entry,
150 |                         "type": "file",
151 |                         "size": size,
152 |                         "extension": ext,
153 |                         "lines": lines
154 |                     }
155 |                     
156 |                     # Calculate complexity for Python files using radon
157 |                     if ext == '.py':
158 |                         try:
159 |                             blocks = cc_visit(content)
160 |                             avg_complexity = sum(b.complexity for b in blocks) / len(blocks) if blocks else 0
161 |                             file_info["complexity"] = avg_complexity
162 |                         except Exception:
163 |                             file_info["complexity"] = estimate_complexity_from_content(content, ext, lines)
164 |                     else:
165 |                         # For all other file types, estimate complexity from code patterns
166 |                         file_info["complexity"] = estimate_complexity_from_content(content, ext, lines)
167 |                     
168 |                     if ext in ['.js', '.ts', '.jsx', '.tsx']:
169 |                         imports, functions = extract_imports_and_functions(content, ext)
170 |                         if imports:
171 |                             file_info["imports"] = imports
172 |                         if functions:
173 |                             file_info["functions"] = functions
174 |                         
175 |                     api_routes = extract_api_routes(content, ext)
176 |                     if api_routes:
177 |                         file_info["api_routes"] = api_routes
178 |                         
179 |                     current_node["children"].append(file_info)
180 |                 except UnicodeDecodeError:
181 |                     # Skip binary files
182 |                     pass
183 |                 except Exception as e:
184 |                     print(f"Error parsing {full_path}: {e}")
185 |                     
186 |     walk_dir(repo_path, file_tree)
187 |     return {"name": os.path.basename(repo_path), "type": "folder", "children": file_tree["children"]}
</content>

 15 |             if risk < 30:

 35 | def build_heatmap_tree(file_tree, current_path=""):
 36 |     children = []
 37 |     for node in file_tree:
 38 |         node_path = f"{current_path}/{node['name']}" if current_path else f"/{node['name']}"
 39 |         if node["type"] == "folder":
 40 |             child_tree = build_heatmap_tree(node["children"], node_path)
 41 |             if child_tree:
 42 |                 children.append({
 43 |                     "name": node["name"],
 44 |                     "path": node_path,
 45 |                     "children": child_tree
 46 |                 })
 47 |         else:
 48 |             complexity, no_test_score, risk, duplication, bugs = _compute_risk_factors(node)
 49 |             lines = node.get("lines", 0)
 50 |             
 51 |             children.append({
 52 |                 "name": node["name"],
 53 |                 "path": node_path,
 54 |                 "value": max(lines, 10),  # D3 tile size based on lines of code
 55 |                 "size": max(lines, 10),
 56 |                 "riskScore": risk,
 57 |                 "riskFactors": {
 58 |                     "complexity": complexity,
 59 |                     "duplication": duplication,
 60 |                     "noTests": no_test_score
 61 |                 },
 62 |                 "bobExplanation": _generate_explanation(node, risk, complexity, no_test_score, duplication),
 63 |                 "issues": _generate_issues(complexity, no_test_score, duplication, lines),
 64 |                 "bugs": bugs
 65 |             })
 66 |     return children
 67 | 
 68 | 
 69 | def _compute_risk_factors(node):
 70 |     """
 71 |     Compute all risk factors for a file node using actual parsed metrics.
 72 |     Returns: (complexity_score, no_test_score, risk_score, duplication_score, bugs)
 73 |     """
 74 |     raw_complexity = node.get("complexity", 1)
 75 |     if not isinstance(raw_complexity, (int, float)):
 76 |         raw_complexity = 1
 77 |     
 78 |     lines = node.get("lines", 0)
 79 |     ext = node.get("extension", "")
 80 |     name = node.get("name", "")
 81 |     num_imports = len(node.get("imports", []))
 82 |     num_functions = len(node.get("functions", []))
 83 |     
 84 |     # --- Complexity Score (0-100) ---
 85 |     # Combine raw complexity with line count and function density
 86 |     # raw_complexity from parser is typically 1-30
 87 |     base_complexity = min(raw_complexity * 4, 60)
 88 |     
 89 |     # Long files are inherently more complex
 90 |     line_complexity = 0
 91 |     if lines > 500:
 92 |         line_complexity = 25
 93 |     elif lines > 300:
 94 |         line_complexity = 18
 95 |     elif lines > 150:
 96 |         line_complexity = 10
 97 |     elif lines > 80:
 98 |         line_complexity = 5
 99 |     
100 |     # Many functions in one file suggests it does too much
101 |     function_density_bonus = min(num_functions * 2, 15)
102 |     
103 |     complexity_score = min(100, int(base_complexity + line_complexity + function_density_bonus))
104 |     
105 |     # --- No Test Score (0-100) ---
106 |     # More nuanced than just checking if "test" is in the name
107 |     is_test_file = any(kw in name.lower() for kw in ['test', 'spec', '__test__', '.test.', '.spec.'])
108 |     is_config = any(kw in name.lower() for kw in ['config', '.env', 'package.json', 'tsconfig', 'webpack', 'vite', 'jest', 'eslint', '.gitignore', 'readme', 'license'])
109 |     is_static = ext in ['.md', '.txt', '.json', '.yaml', '.yml', '.toml', '.xml', '.csv', '.svg', '.ico']
110 |     
111 |     if is_test_file:
112 |         no_test_score = 0  # This IS a test file
113 |     elif is_config or is_static:
114 |         no_test_score = 10  # Config/static files don't really need tests
115 |     elif ext in ['.css', '.scss', '.less', '.html', '.htm']:
116 |         no_test_score = 15  # Style/markup files rarely need unit tests
117 |     else:
118 |         # Code files that aren't tests get penalized based on complexity
119 |         no_test_score = min(100, 30 + int(complexity_score * 0.5))
120 |     
121 |     # --- Duplication Score (0-100) ---
122 |     # Estimate duplication from import count and file size patterns
123 |     if lines == 0:
124 |         duplication = 0
125 |     else:
126 |         # Files with many imports relative to their size tend to have less duplication
127 |         import_ratio = num_imports / max(lines, 1) * 100
128 |         base_dup = max(0, 30 - int(import_ratio * 5))  # More imports = less duplication
129 |         
130 |         # Longer files tend to have more duplication
131 |         length_dup = min(40, int(math.log2(max(lines, 1)) * 4))
132 |         
133 |         # Factor in complexity: high complexity + long files = likely duplication
134 |         complexity_dup = int(complexity_score * 0.2)
135 |         
136 |         duplication = min(100, base_dup + length_dup + complexity_dup)
137 |     
138 |     # --- Overall Risk Score (0-100) ---
139 |     # Weighted combination of all factors
140 |     risk = int(
141 |         complexity_score * 0.40 +   # 40% weight on complexity
142 |         no_test_score * 0.30 +      # 30% weight on test coverage
143 |         duplication * 0.20 +         # 20% weight on duplication
144 |         line_complexity * 0.10       # 10% extra from file length
145 |     )
146 |     risk = min(max(risk, 0), 100)
147 |     
148 |     # --- Bug Estimation ---
149 |     # Based on risk and complexity
150 |     if risk < 20:
151 |         bugs = 0
152 |     elif risk < 40:
153 |         bugs = 1
154 |     elif risk < 60:
155 |         bugs = max(1, int(complexity_score / 25))
156 |     elif risk < 80:
157 |         bugs = max(2, int(complexity_score / 15))
158 |     else:
159 |         bugs = max(3, int(complexity_score / 10))
160 |     
161 |     return complexity_score, no_test_score, risk, duplication, bugs
162 | 
163 | 
164 | def _generate_explanation(node, risk, complexity, no_test_score, duplication):
165 |     """Generate a meaningful explanation based on actual file metrics."""
166 |     name = node.get("name", "")
167 |     lines = node.get("lines", 0)
168 |     num_functions = len(node.get("functions", []))
169 |     
170 |     parts = []
171 |     
172 |     if risk >= 70:
173 |         parts.append(f"High-risk file (score: {risk}/100).")
174 |     elif risk >= 40:
175 |         parts.append(f"Moderate risk (score: {risk}/100).")
176 |     else:
177 |         parts.append(f"Low risk (score: {risk}/100).")
178 |     
179 |     if complexity > 60:
180 |         parts.append(f"High cyclomatic complexity ({complexity}) suggests deeply nested control flow.")
181 |     elif complexity > 30:
182 |         parts.append(f"Moderate complexity ({complexity}) with room for simplification.")
183 |     
184 |     if lines > 300:
185 |         parts.append(f"Large file ({lines} lines) — consider splitting into smaller modules.")
186 |     
187 |     if num_functions > 10:
188 |         parts.append(f"Contains {num_functions} functions — may have too many responsibilities.")
189 |     
190 |     if no_test_score > 60:
191 |         parts.append("No corresponding test file detected.")
192 |     
193 |     if duplication > 50:
194 |         parts.append("Potential code duplication detected.")
195 |     
196 |     if not parts:
197 |         parts.append("Well-structured file with acceptable metrics.")
198 |     
199 |     return " ".join(parts)
200 | 
201 | 
202 | def _generate_issues(complexity, no_test_score, duplication, lines):
203 |     """Generate a list of specific issues based on metrics."""
204 |     issues = []
205 |     
206 |     if complexity > 60:
207 |         issues.append("High cyclomatic complexity")
208 |     elif complexity > 35:
209 |         issues.append("Moderate complexity")
210 |     
211 |     if no_test_score > 60:
212 |         issues.append("Missing test coverage")
213 |     
214 |     if duplication > 50:
215 |         issues.append("Potential code duplication")
216 |     
217 |     if lines > 500:
218 |         issues.append("File too large — split recommended")
219 |     elif lines > 300:
220 |         issues.append("Large file")
221 |     
222 |     if not issues:
223 |         issues.append("No major issues")
224 |     
225 |     return issues

## 🎯 Most Complex Function: `compute_scale_profile()`

**Location:** [`scale_simulator.py:10-118`](codePulse-clean/backend/services/scale_simulator.py:10)

**Complexity:** This is the most complex function in the codebase due to:
- 109 lines of code
- Multiple mathematical calculations
- 7 helper function calls
- Complex business logic with 15+ variables
- Multi-dimensional data transformations

---

## 📋 Step-by-Step Breakdown

### **Purpose**
Predicts how a system will behave under increasing load by analyzing actual code metrics (complexity, dependencies, risk) and generating infrastructure scaling characteristics.

---

### **Step 1: Extract Input Metrics** (Lines 16-30)
```python
num_api_routes = len(api_routes)           # Count API endpoints
num_deps = len(dependency_graph["links"])  # Count dependencies
num_nodes = len(dependency_graph["nodes"]) # Count modules
```

**What it does:**
- Counts API routes, dependency links, and nodes from input data
- Filters high-risk files (risk ≥ 60) and critical files (risk ≥ 80)
- Calculates average and maximum risk scores
- Computes **coupling ratio** = dependencies ÷ nodes (measures interconnectedness)

**Example:**
```
Input: 50 API routes, 200 dependencies, 100 nodes
Output: coupling_ratio = 200/100 = 2.0 (highly coupled)
```

---

### **Step 2: Detect Architectural Components** (Line 32)
```python
components = _detect_components(heatmap_files, api_routes)
```

**What it does:**
- Groups files by top-level directory (e.g., `src/`, `api/`, `models/`)
- Classifies each group into component types:
  - `database` (models, schemas)
  - `api_gateway` (routes, controllers)
  - `auth_service` (auth, jwt, session)
  - `business_logic` (services, core)
  - `frontend` (components, views)
  - etc.
- Calculates average complexity and risk per component

**Example:**
```
Input: Files in /api/ directory
Output: {
  name: "API Gateway",
  type: "api_gateway",
  fileCount: 15,
  avgComplexity: 45.2,
  avgRisk: 62.1
}
```

---

### **Step 3: Calculate Base Latency** (Lines 36-42)
```python
base_latency = max(15, min(200, int(
    20 +                              # minimum network overhead
    avg_complexity * 0.8 +            # complexity overhead
    num_api_routes * 0.5 +            # route matching overhead
    coupling_ratio * 5 +              # dependency resolution
    len(high_risk_files) * 0.3        # tech debt drag
)))
```

**What it does:**
- Estimates baseline response time in milliseconds
- Higher complexity → more processing → higher latency
- More API routes → more routing overhead
- High coupling → more dependency resolution time
- Clamped between 15ms (minimum) and 200ms (maximum)

**Example:**
```
avg_complexity = 40
num_api_routes = 50
coupling_ratio = 2.0
high_risk_files = 30

base_latency = 20 + (40*0.8) + (50*0.5) + (2.0*5) + (30*0.3)
             = 20 + 32 + 25 + 10 + 9
             = 96ms
```

---

### **Step 4: Calculate Base Error Rate** (Lines 45-50)
```python
base_error_rate = max(0.01, min(5.0, round(
    0.01 +                            # baseline
    (avg_risk / 100) * 0.5 +          # risk-proportional errors
    len(critical_files) * 0.05 +      # critical file hotspots
    (1 if avg_complexity > 60 else 0) * 0.3
, 2)))
```

**What it does:**
- Estimates percentage of requests that will fail
- Higher risk → more errors
- Critical files (risk > 80) significantly increase error rate
- Very complex code (>60) adds penalty

**Example:**
```
avg_risk = 55
critical_files = 5
avg_complexity = 65

base_error_rate = 0.01 + (55/100)*0.5 + (5*0.05) + 0.3
                = 0.01 + 0.275 + 0.25 + 0.3
                = 0.835% error rate
```

---

### **Step 5: Calculate Base Memory Usage** (Lines 53-58)
```python
base_memory = max(10, min(50, int(
    15 +                              # base runtime
    total_files * 0.05 +              # per-file module load
    num_deps * 0.15 +                 # dependency memory
    avg_complexity * 0.1              # runtime state overhead
)))
```

**What it does:**
- Estimates baseline memory consumption as percentage
- More files → more modules loaded → more memory
- More dependencies → more memory for dependency graph
- Clamped between 10% and 50%

**Example:**
```
total_files = 500
num_deps = 200
avg_complexity = 40

base_memory = 15 + (500*0.05) + (200*0.15) + (40*0.1)
            = 15 + 25 + 30 + 4
            = 74 → clamped to 50%
```

---

### **Step 6: Compute Component Failure Thresholds** (Lines 62-77)
```python
for comp in components:
    threshold = _compute_failure_threshold(comp, coupling_ratio, avg_risk)
    component_profiles.append({...})

component_profiles.sort(key=lambda c: c["failureThreshold"])
```

**What it does:**
- For each component, calculates the **traffic multiplier** at which it fails
- Formula considers:
  - Complexity penalty: -0.3x per complexity point
  - Risk penalty: -0.2x per risk point
  - File count penalty: -0.5x per file
  - Coupling penalty: -3x coupling ratio
  - Type bonus: static assets +30, database -10
- Sorts components by failure threshold (weakest first)

**Example:**
```
Component: API Gateway
- avgComplexity: 45 → penalty: 45 * 0.3 = 13.5
- avgRisk: 62 → penalty: 62 * 0.2 = 12.4
- fileCount: 15 → penalty: 15 * 0.5 = 7.5
- coupling_ratio: 2.0 → penalty: 2.0 * 3 = 6
- type_bonus: -5 (API gateway inherently stressed)

threshold = 90 - 13.5 - 12.4 - 7.5 - 6 - 5 = 45.6x
(Fails at 45.6x normal traffic)
```

---

### **Step 7: Calculate Growth Rates** (Lines 81-91)
```python
latency_growth_rate = max(3, min(20, int(
    5 + avg_complexity * 0.15 + coupling_ratio * 2
)))

error_growth_rate = max(0.05, min(0.5, round(
    0.08 + (avg_risk / 100) * 0.15 + len(critical_files) * 0.01
, 3)))

memory_growth_rate = max(0.3, min(1.5, round(
    0.4 + total_files * 0.002 + num_deps * 0.005
, 2)))
```

**What it does:**
- Defines how quickly metrics degrade as traffic increases
- **Latency growth**: How many ms added per traffic multiplier
- **Error growth**: How much error rate increases per multiplier
- **Memory growth**: How much memory % increases per multiplier

**Example:**
```
At 1x traffic: 96ms latency, 0.84% errors, 50% memory
At 10x traffic: 96 + (10*8) = 176ms, 0.84 + (10*0.12) = 2.04%, 50 + (10*0.8) = 58%
At 45x traffic: System fails (API Gateway threshold reached)
```

---

### **Step 8: Generate Recommendations** (Lines 94-97)
```python
recommendations = _generate_recommendations(
    components, component_profiles, avg_complexity, 
    avg_risk, num_api_routes, coupling_ratio, total_files, languages
)
```

**What it does:**
- Analyzes all computed metrics
- Generates actionable recommendations:
  - "Strengthen [weakest component]" if threshold < 30
  - "Reduce Module Coupling" if coupling_ratio > 2.0
  - "Implement API Rate Limiting" if routes > 10
  - "Refactor High-Complexity Modules" if avg_complexity > 40
  - "Implement Code Splitting" if total_files > 200
- Prioritizes by severity (critical > warning > info)

---

### **Step 9: Return Complete Profile** (Lines 99-118)
```python
return {
    "baseLatency": 96,
    "baseErrorRate": 0.84,
    "baseMemory": 50,
    "latencyGrowthRate": 8,
    "errorGrowthRate": 0.12,
    "memoryGrowthRate": 0.8,
    "components": [...],  # Top 6 weakest components
    "recommendations": [...],  # Top 3 recommendations
    "summary": {...}  # Overall metrics
}
```

2. **Mathematical Modeling**: Complex formulas with weights and penalties
3. **Recursive Processing**: Calls 4 helper functions that process nested data
4. **Business Logic**: Translates code metrics into infrastructure predictions
5. **Data Transformation**: Converts raw analysis into actionable insights

**Cyclomatic Complexity:** ~15 (multiple conditionals in helper functions)
**Lines of Code:** 109 (main function) + 246 (helper functions) = 355 total
**Dependencies:** 7 helper functions, math module, multiple data structures

This function is the "brain" of the scale simulation feature, turning static code analysis into dynamic performance predictions.
